The first implementation updated `config_` correctly from either group but never pushed the new value onto the *other* group's own sibling widget - invisible while only one group is ever shown at a time, but a real, user-visible staleness the instant the `FilterType` combo switches between the two (the newly-edited value would silently revert to whatever the other widget last displayed). Caught by `changingDynamicSpeckleDensityAndIntensityUpdateTheSameFieldsAsSpeckleAdd()`, written to check exactly this cross-widget sync rather than just each group's own `FilterConfiguration` effect in isolation. **Fixed** by having each change handler push its own new value directly onto its sibling widget too, `QSignalBlocker`-guarded so the sibling's own re-`setValue()` doesn't re-trigger its own handler and redundantly re-emit `filterConfigurationChanged()`.
    - **New tests**: `test_filter_configuration.cpp` (Core, 6 new cases - defaults for all ten new scalar fields, `noiseSeed()`'s own freshly-random-per-instance property, every setter, every new `FilterType` settable, JSON round-trip, and lenient-load-from-a-pre-installment-project-file defaults). `test_filter_application.cpp` (Core, 26 new cases across all eight types - no-op edge cases, hand-derived exact values for `Denoise`'s soft knee and `FeedbackDistortion`'s decay and `SpectralWavefold`'s fold points, determinism checks for `SpeckleAdd`/`GranularNoise` including a different-seed-different-pattern case, and a genuinely-different-between-calls check for `DynamicSpeckle`). `test_filter_configuration_panel.cpp` (Studio, 11 new cases - all eight groups' own visibility, every new spin box's own change-and-emit behavior, `setFilterConfiguration()` syncing every new control, and the cross-widget sync fix above). Full regression: sound-mind-core 561/561 (up from 529), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings.

103. **Deferred Filters, Installment B: the rest of Tonal (Channel Balance, Invert) plus the rest of Spectral shaping (Convolve), closing out both families, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "Filter Layer".** Settled in `v0.0.36.2`. Unlike Installment A's Noise & distortion (a family the design doc named no legacy precedent for at all), a sibling Python codebase implementation existed for all three of these - confirmed with the user before any code that using it as the authoritative source (the same research-first approach Decision #101's Warp already established) was the right call rather than guessing fresh, and it surfaced one real, worth-flagging discrepancy.
    - **Channel Balance is an energy-conserving pan law in *linear* amplitude, confirmed with the user against legacy's own `channel_balance()`, over the more obvious-sounding independent-per-channel-gain alternative**: `total = left + right` (converted from dB via a locally-duplicated `dbToLinearAmplitude()`/`linearAmplitudeToDb()` pair - Decision #59's own "duplicated, not shared" precedent, since `compositor.cpp`'s own copies are `src/`-private), then `left = total * (1 - balance)`, `right = total * balance`. The only filter type that genuinely mixes the two channels together rather than processing each independently (so it doesn't go through `applyPerChannelGridFilter()`), and the only one whose own "no-op" value (`balance = 0.5`) depends on the input (only a no-op when `left == right` already) rather than being a fixed, input-independent identity the way every other filter's own no-op value is.
    - **Invert's own real meaning was the one genuine discrepancy worth flagging, not silently resolved either way**: legacy's `invert_filter()` is `out = 1 - normalized_amplitude` - an amplitude negative (quiet becomes loud, loud becomes quiet), *not* audio polarity/phase inversion (`out = -in` on a signed sample), which "Invert" could just as reasonably suggest to someone who hadn't read the legacy source. Flagged explicitly and confirmed with the user, who chose legacy's own meaning - which, on reflection, is also the operation actually consistent with every other filter type in this codebase (all of them already operate in the normalized-amplitude/dB spectrogram domain, never on raw time-domain samples - a polarity flip would have been the one inconsistent outlier). No parameters of its own.
    - **Convolve is full legacy parity, confirmed with the user over two narrower options** (a fixed-size grid with no presets/library; a configurable size with no presets/library): a configurable odd kernel size (`convolveKernelSize()`, forced odd via `| 1` matching `medianBlur2D()`'s own precedent), an editable kernel (`convolveKernel()`, row-major), eight classic built-in presets (Identity, Sharpen, Edge Detect, Emboss, Box Blur, Gaussian Blur, Sobel X, Sobel Y - matching legacy's own `CONVOLVE_PRESETS` exactly), positive-coefficient-sum normalization (`convolveNormalize()`), and a dry/wet mix (`convolveAmount()`). The core algorithm (`convolve2D()`) is a plain 2D spatial convolution, clamp-to-edge boundary handling (matching every other spatial filter in this file), ported directly from legacy's own `custom_convolve_filter()` (`scipy.ndimage.convolve(..., mode="nearest")`) - the design doc's own "custom spectral or temporal responses" phrasing is this one mechanism doing double duty by kernel shape/orientation (a horizontal-only kernel acts mostly across time, a vertical-only one mostly across frequency), not two separate implementations, matching legacy exactly.
    - **Named kernel save/load reuses the "peer resource library on `Project`" pattern `NamedMindShot`/`NamedMindGrain`/`NamedMindWave` already establish, confirmed with the user over porting legacy's own per-kernel `.npy`-file-under-a-project-folder convention**: a new `NamedConvolutionKernel` struct (`convolution_kernel.h`, new file) plus `Project::addConvolutionKernel()`/`removeConvolutionKernel()`/`convolutionKernelById()`/`convolutionKernels()`, mirroring `NamedMindShot`'s own exact shape (fresh-max-id-plus-one assignment, lenient JSON load for a pre-Installment-B project file). Loading a saved kernel into a Filter layer's own `FilterConfiguration` is a one-time copy, not a persistent binding - there is no "this Filter layer is linked to kernel #3" relationship anywhere, so editing the loaded copy afterward never mutates the library entry, and removing the library entry never affects a Filter layer that already loaded it.
    - **The Filter Configuration Panel's own Preset combo and Load Kernel combo are both one-shot triggers, not sticky selections, confirmed with the user**: selecting either immediately writes new values into the kernel grid and then resets its own combo back to a leading placeholder item - the loaded/preset values are just a starting point for further hand-editing, not a persistent link back to that preset or saved entry, the same reasoning behind the one-time-copy contract above.
    - **A real bug found by a test written for exactly this scenario, not by inspection**: `rebuildConvolveKernelGrid()`'s first implementation destroyed the outgoing size's own spin boxes via `QObject::deleteLater()` before creating the new size's own replacements (with the same object names, `convolveKernelSpinBox_<row>_<col>`). `deleteLater()` only *schedules* destruction for the next event-loop iteration, so the old widgets were still technically alive and still findable by name until then - any synchronous `findChild()` lookup immediately after a size change (exactly what every test here does, and what a real caller checking the panel's own state right after `setValue()` would also do) found the stale, about-to-be-destroyed widget instead of its replacement. **Fixed** by deleting the old widgets immediately (`delete spinBox`, after `removeWidget()` had already detached them from the layout) rather than deferring it - safe here since the rebuild is never triggered from inside one of the widgets being replaced.
    - **New tests**: `test_convolution_kernel.cpp` (Core, new file, 2 cases - fresh-identity defaults, JSON round-trip). `test_project.cpp` (Core, 8 new cases - the convolution kernel library's own add/remove/find/JSON-round-trip/lenient-load lifecycle, mirroring `NamedMindShot`'s own existing test block exactly). `test_filter_configuration.cpp` (Core, 6 new cases). `test_filter_application.cpp` (Core, 15 new cases - Channel Balance's own energy-conservation and both-extremes cases, Invert's own self-inverse-at-midpoint case, Convolve's own hand-computed box-blur average, a normalize-vs-pre-divided-kernel equivalence case, and a malformed-kernel-length no-op case). `test_filter_configuration_panel.cpp` (Studio, 13 new cases - all three groups' own visibility, the kernel grid's own dynamic rebuild-on-size-change behavior, preset/load-kernel one-shot-trigger behavior, the save button's own emitted signal, and the cross-widget sync fix above). Full regression: sound-mind-core 593/593 (up from 561), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings.

104. **Deferred Filters, Installment C: Displace and Channel Cycle, closing out Geometric, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "Filter Layer".** Settled in `v0.0.36.3`. Continues Installment B's own research-first pattern (Decision #103): a legacy Python Studio implementation existed for both, confirmed with the user as the authoritative source before any code.
    - **`Displace` reuses `DirectionalBlur`'s own angle convention exactly, confirmed with the user rather than re-deriving one from legacy's own row-major image layout**: `0`° shifts along the time axis (columns), `90`° along the frequency axis (bins) - `directionalBlurAngleDegrees()`'s own docs already establish this, and legacy's own `offset_filter()` formula (`output(row, col) = input(row - distance*sin(angle), col - distance*cos(angle))`) maps onto it directly once the axis labels are matched up rather than assumed. Bilinear sampling (`sampleBilinear()`, new) at the shifted source position, exactly matching legacy's own `scipy.ndimage.map_coordinates(order=1)`.
    - **Two real, confirmed departures from legacy's own exact behavior**: legacy fills anything sourced from outside the image with silence; this codebase clamps to the nearest edge instead, for consistency with every other spatial filter already built here (Blur, Sharpen, Convolve all clamp). Legacy also has an optional "apply to phase" toggle; this installment leaves phase always untouched, matching every filter type built so far, rather than making `Displace` the first to conditionally touch it. Both confirmed with the user directly rather than silently picked either way - the recommended, precedent-matching option was chosen both times.
    - **`ChannelCycle` is a deliberate simplification of legacy's own `color_rotate()`, not a straight port, confirmed with the user**: legacy operates on 3 of 4 independent "pages" (left amplitude, right amplitude, left phase, right phase) selected by one of 9 named "RGB mapping" presets, itself an artifact of legacy's own image-display architecture (visualizing 4 data pages as a 3-channel color image). This codebase has no separate left/right phase - only one `sharedPhaseRadians` - so there is exactly one meaningful set of 3 channels to cycle (left loudness, right loudness, phase), and no mapping selector is needed at all. The underlying continuous-angle rotation math is otherwise unchanged: `0`°/`360`° is identity, every `120`° is one full step, a fractional angle linearly interpolates between adjacent steps.
    - **A genuine translation decision beyond the simplification above, reasoned through rather than asked, since it follows directly from how legacy's own data is represented**: legacy's "pages" are already stored as normalized `[0, 1]` values regardless of what they represent (amplitude or phase alike, since they're all just image pixels) - rotating/blending them together is dimensionally consistent in legacy's own domain. This codebase stores amplitude in dB (`-96..0`) and phase in radians - two incompatible numeric ranges - so `applyChannelCycle()` normalizes all three to a shared `[0, 1]` domain first (amplitude via the existing `dbToUnit()`, phase via wrapping into a `[0, 1]` turn fraction), performs the rotation there, then converts each destination channel back to its own native domain. Without this step, blending a dB value directly with a radian value would be numerically meaningless rather than merely a wild creative effect (which cycling amplitude/phase *is* meant to be, per the design doc's own framing - the normalization keeps that effect coherent rather than accidentally broken).
    - **The `FilterType` selector's own family order was corrected while adding these two**: `docs/sound-mind-design.md` lists Geometric *before* Tonal and Spectral shaping, but Installments A/B's own combo entries had implicitly left room only at the end (after `SpectralWavefold`, before `ToneCurve`) - which happens to be the correct slot once Geometric's own place in the family order is checked directly against the design doc rather than assumed from where the two prior installments happened to leave off.
    - **New tests**: `test_filter_configuration.cpp` (Core, 5 new cases). `test_filter_application.cpp` (Core, 10 new cases - Displace's own zero-distance no-op, exact integer shifts along both axes, a hand-verified bilinear-interpolation case, and phase-untouched; Channel Cycle's own identity-at-0°-and-360° cases, a hand-derived exact full-step rotation at 120°, and a hand-derived halfway interpolation at 60°). `test_filter_configuration_panel.cpp` (Studio, 5 new cases - both groups' own visibility and change-and-emit behavior, `setFilterConfiguration()` syncing both). Full regression: sound-mind-core 607/607 (up from 593), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings. (One unrelated, pre-existing GPU performance-timing test - `mixAmplitudePhaseSignal is measurably faster than the CPU reference on a large signal`, in `sound-mind-gpu`'s own suite, untouched by this installment - failed once under load during a full-suite run and passed cleanly in isolation immediately after; a flaky wall-clock benchmark, not a regression from this installment's own changes.)

105. **Deferred Filters, Installment D: Spectral Reverb, closing out Space and the entire `v0.Y.36.1` milestone, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "Filter Layer".** Settled in `v0.0.36.4`. Continues the same research-first pattern every prior `v0.Y.36.1` installment used from Installment B onward: a legacy Python Studio implementation (`reverb_filter()`, plus a dialog-level phase-diffusion add-on) existed, confirmed with the user as the authoritative source before any code, with one scope question (whether to build the dialog-level phase-diffusion feature too) resolved in favor of the core filter alone.
    - **The single largest, most involved algorithm this milestone built**, ported in four ordered stages, confirmed with the user before implementation: absorption (a per-bin dB attenuation, `0` at the lowest encoded frequency ramping to `-24dB * absorption` at the highest - real rooms absorb high frequencies fastest), diffusion (a Gaussian blur along the frequency axis only, `blurAlongFrequencyAxis()`, new), convolution (each bin's own time series convolved with an exponentially-decaying impulse response), and a final dry/wet mix.
    - **A genuine, load-bearing domain split within one filter, reasoned through rather than silently picked**: absorption and diffusion both operate directly in dB space, matching `UniformBlur`'s/every other spatial filter's own established precedent of blurring/attenuating dB values directly. The impulse-response convolution itself, though, is done in *linear* amplitude (`dbToLinearAmplitude()`/`linearAmplitudeToDb()`, the same pair `ChannelBalance` - Decision #103 - already introduced): summing multiple delayed, decaying copies of a signal *is* the literal definition of a reverb tail, a physical superposition that's only meaningful in linear amplitude - the identical "dB values can't be meaningfully summed directly" reasoning `ChannelBalance` already established, now recurring in a second, unrelated filter type. Absorption/diffusion staying dB-domain while the convolution alone switches to linear is deliberate, not an inconsistency - each stage uses whichever domain is actually correct for what it's doing.
    - **The impulse response's own RT60 (`-60`dB) definition is exact, not approximate**: `ln(1000) ≈ 6.9078` nepers is exactly `-60`dB, so `buildReverbImpulseResponse()`'s own exponential decay lands precisely on that point at the end of its own (room-size-scaled) decay length, matching legacy's own RT60 definition exactly. Normalized so its own coefficients sum to `~1` (an energy-neutral tail), also matching legacy exactly.
    - **A causal-convolution boundary condition, deliberately distinct from every spatial filter's own clamp-to-edge convention established since `v0.Y.28.1`**: a source position before frame `0` (before the recording's own start) contributes silence to the convolution sum, not a clamped edge value - there is nothing "before the start" of a recording to echo, the standard convolution boundary condition, not a spatial one. Confirmed as the correct read rather than assumed identical to how every other filter in this file already handles its own boundary.
    - **This codebase's own bin convention (`bin 0` = lowest encoded frequency) is the exact opposite of legacy's own image row convention (`row 0` = highest frequency), confirmed and adjusted for rather than copied verbatim**: legacy's absorption curve ramps from `(1 - absorption)` at row `0` (its own highest frequency) to `1` at the last row (its own lowest frequency); this codebase's own `buildReverbAbsorptionCurveDb()` ramps from `0`dB at bin `0` (the lowest encoded frequency) to `-24dB * absorption` at the last bin (the highest) - the same physical intent (dampen highs more), re-derived against this codebase's own axis direction rather than assumed to match legacy's array indices literally.
    - **New tests**: `test_filter_configuration.cpp` (Core, 5 new cases). `test_filter_application.cpp` (Core, 6 new cases - a mix-0 no-op regardless of every other parameter, the exact per-bin absorption curve via a trivial single-tap impulse response, silence during pre-delay, a hand-derived exact decay across two frames following pre-delay, and phase-untouched). `test_filter_configuration_panel.cpp` (Studio, 4 new cases - the group's own visibility, all six controls' own change-and-emit behavior, and `setFilterConfiguration()` syncing all six). Full regression: sound-mind-core 617/617 (up from 607), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings.

106. **Deferred Blend Modes, closing out `v0.Y.37.1`, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s own explicit "none is designed yet."** Settled in `v0.0.37.1`. Scoped via five rounds of confirming questions before any code (mode set, domain, phase handling, installment scope, Paste's own UI surface), plus a sixth round after discovering a real discrepancy against the legacy Python Studio's own blend engine - the same research-first pattern every prior multi-installment milestone this project has built established, here compressed into a single installment rather than several.

    - **Seven `BlendMode` values, not the design doc's own open-ended "usual image-editor catalogue"**: `Normal` and `Overwrite` name this codebase's own two pre-existing behaviors precisely (layer compositing's own already-shipped `v0.Y.27.1` audio-style summing, and Paste/Mind Shot's own already-shipped hard, unconditional overwrite) - confirmed with the user as *not* new modes so much as existing behavior finally being named and made one selectable option among several, each staying the default for its own domain (layers default to `Normal`, Paste/Mind Shot/Mind Grain default to `Overwrite`) so a project saved before this milestone reproduces its own prior behavior exactly. `Multiply`/`Screen`/`Overlay`/`Difference`/`Add` are the "classic five," confirmed as this milestone's own scope over legacy's full 13-mode set (Color Burn/Dodge, Min/Max, Reflect, Glow, Negation, XOR remain a documented future-work item).
    - **A real, deliberate domain split confirmed with the user rather than silently picked**: `Normal` stays in this codebase's own already-shipped *linear-amplitude* domain (audio-style mixing, summing real acoustic energy - not an image-editor-style pixel operation, per the design doc's own explicit framing) - untouched by this milestone. The five new modes all operate in `dbToUnit()`-normalized space instead, matching legacy's own pixel-normalized domain and every other "image-editor style" filter already built here (`Invert`, `Convolve`, `BitDepthCrush`, `SpectralWavefold`) - confirmed with the user over forcing them into `Normal`'s own linear-amplitude domain. `Overwrite` needs neither domain at all - a hard, unconditional copy, opacity ignored entirely.
    - **A real discrepancy surfaced against the legacy Python Studio's own blend engine (`image_ops/blend.py`'s `_blend_onto()`), flagged rather than silently resolved**: legacy's own `"normal"` mode is literally `blended_amp = b` - classic image alpha-over, completely different from this codebase's own already-shipped `Normal` (audio-style linear-amplitude summing). This codebase's `Normal` was **not** changed to match legacy's own `"normal"` - the design doc's own explicit "not image-style alpha-over" framing for `Normal` predates and overrides legacy's own naming coincidence. Legacy's own `"overwrite"` mode (`eff_opacity = 1.0` always, `blended_amp = b`), by contrast, matches Paste/Mind Shot's own pre-existing hard-overwrite behavior exactly - ported as `BlendMode::Overwrite` with no discrepancy at all.
    - **Phase ports legacy's own exact per-mode formula, confirmed with the user over a uniform treatment across every mode**: `Multiply`'s phases add; `Screen`/`Overlay` use an amplitude-weighted circular mean of the two inputs' own complex values (falling back to `base`'s own phase if that sum is negligibly small), `Overlay` additionally region-gated between `Multiply`-style phase (`base < 0.5`) and `Screen`-style phase, the same branch point its own amplitude formula uses; `Difference`/`Add` use the complex *difference*/*sum* of the two inputs directly, decoupled from the amplitude result above (a real, intentional legacy behavior, not a shortcut - see `applyBlendedCell()`'s own docs for the "Difference cancels to silence while phase still reflects real complex interference" case this decouples, an amplitude-space-only computation would miss entirely). Every mode's own resulting complex value is then crossfaded against `base`'s own original complex value by `opacity` - a second, phase-space crossfade, independent of the amplitude crossfade, also matching legacy exactly.
    - **One deliberate, reasoned deviation from a literal legacy port**: legacy always weights each side's phase contribution by its own first amplitude channel alone (an artifact of legacy's own generic n-page loop, not a considered choice). This codebase weights by `(left + right) / 2`'s own unit-normalized magnitude instead - confirmed with the user as the better fit, since it matches this codebase's own already-shipped `Normal`-mode phase derivation convention (`compositor.cpp`'s own pre-existing `mid = (newLeft + newRight) / 2`) rather than introducing a second, inconsistent phase-weighting rule for the five new modes alone.
    - **One shared `applyBlendedCell()` function is the single implementation behind all three consumers** (layer compositing, Paste, Mind Shot/Mind Grain stamping) - confirmed with the user as this milestone's own scope over layer compositing alone, closing the same real gap (`v0.Y.33.1`'s Mind Shot stamping and `v0.Y.25.1`'s Paste were both, until now, a hard, Normal-only overwrite with no blend-mode choice of their own) the roadmap's own entry had already flagged. `compositor.cpp`'s `mixLayerInto()` was expected, going in, to need rewriting into a sequential bottom-to-top fold to make this possible - direct code reading found it already *is* one (`mixLayerInto()` already runs once per contributing layer in a loop), so the actual remaining work was narrower than planned: only the per-layer blend *formula* needed to become configurable, not the fold's own control flow.
    - **The GPU path (`ComputeDevice::mixAmplitudePhaseSignal()`) stays `Normal`-only** - every other blend mode always takes the CPU path in `compositor.cpp`'s `mixLayerIntoGpuOrCpu()`, matching this codebase's established "CPU first, GPU deferred" precedent for new filter/blend math (every Filter Layer type built in `v0.Y.36.1` did the same). `compositeProject()`'s own single-layer fast path (`compositeSingleLayer()`) is excluded whenever the sole contributor's blend mode isn't `Normal` too - that fast path's own "single-layer reduces to itself" shortcut is a `Normal`-specific algebraic identity (summing one term onto silence); `Multiply` against silence is silence, not the layer's own raw content, so every other mode needs the general path's own real `applyBlendedCell()` call.
    - **Paste gets a new "Paste Blend Mode" dropdown in the Selection Configuration panel, not a panel of its own** - confirmed with the user: Paste has no tool-configuration panel to add a control to (it's driven by Edit → Paste, not a paintable tool type), and the Selection Configuration panel is already the closest thing to a "what does the next selection-driven action do" surface. Mind Shot/Mind Grain instead get a single shared `blendModeCombo_` in the Tool Configuration Panel's own shared-controls area (`sharedControlsForm_`), shown only for those two types - the exact inverse of the existing Falloff/Size/Color/Opacity visibility rule those two already established (Decision #98) - rather than duplicating the same combo once per type-specific group, even though `blendMode()` is declared separately on each of `MindShotConfiguration`/`MindGrainConfiguration` (not a shared `ToolConfiguration` base member).
    - **A sixth, mid-implementation scope question surfaced and resolved with the user**: `Layer` gained real Core support for `blendMode()` (accessor, JSON, compositor dispatch) as part of this milestone, but `LayersPanel`'s own class docs had explicitly deferred any blend-mode UI to the separate, already-planned Layers Panel Redesign milestone (`v0.Y.44.1`, one of that milestone's own five named additions). Confirmed with the user to add a minimal, plain Blend Mode combo to the current `LayersPanel` now anyway (one row per non-Background layer, next to the opacity/transform controls) rather than shipping the Core feature with no reachable control surface until a separate, later milestone lands - this milestone's own roadmap Demo text ("switch a layer from Normal to Multiply and hear/see the difference") needed a working UI today, not just a working file format. The later redesign still replaces this with its own polished accordion-row control; this one isn't meant to survive that redesign unchanged. `LayerController` gained a sixth undoable property setter (`setLayerBlendMode()`), following the exact same apply/undo/redo shape as its existing five.
    - **A MindWave-bound blend mode - the design doc's own other named candidate ("making *where* and *how* layers mix spatially selective") - was not built**: none of the five confirmed scoping questions this milestone's own planning pass asked included it, so it stays out of scope rather than being silently added or silently dropped - a real future-work item, not an oversight, flagged in the roadmap's own updated entry.
    - **New files**: `sound-mind-core/include/sound_mind/core/blend_mode.h` (the `BlendMode` enum), `sound-mind-core/include/sound_mind/core/blend_mode_application.h`/`src/blend_mode_application.cpp` (`BlendedCell`, `applyBlendedCell()`).
    - **New tests**: `test_blend_mode_application.cpp` (Core, new file, 17 cases - every mode's own hand-verified amplitude formula, Normal's own linear-amplitude-sum behavior matching `compositor.cpp`'s pre-existing formula exactly, Overwrite's verbatim-copy-ignoring-opacity behavior, and phase edge cases including Difference's own amplitude-cancels-but-phase-doesn't case and the silent-result-falls-back-to-base-phase case). `test_layer.cpp` (Core, 3 new cases). `test_compositor.cpp` (Core, 4 new cases - the fast-path exclusion, general-path Multiply dispatch, and GPU/CPU agreement for a non-Normal mode). `test_paste_operation.cpp` (Core, 3 new cases). `test_operation_log.cpp` (Core, 2 new cases - non-default round-trip and lenient old-format load). `test_tool_configuration.cpp` (Core, 4 new cases). `test_paste_application.cpp`/`test_paint_application.cpp` (Core, 1 new case each - non-Overwrite dispatch). `test_selection_configuration_panel.cpp` (Studio, 2 new cases). `test_tool_configuration_panel.cpp` (Studio, 5 new cases). `test_layers_panel.cpp` (Studio, 3 new cases). `test_layer_controller.cpp` (Studio, 2 new cases). `test_main_window.cpp` (Studio, 1 new case - Paste's own end-to-end blend mode wiring). Full regression: sound-mind-core 651/651 (up from 617), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings. (One unrelated, pre-existing GPU performance-timing test - `mixAmplitudePhaseSignal is measurably faster than the CPU reference on a large signal`, in `sound-mind-gpu`'s own suite, untouched by this milestone - failed intermittently under system load during full-suite runs and passed cleanly in isolation and in a clean full run alike; a flaky wall-clock benchmark, not a regression from this milestone's own changes.)

107. **Filter Parameter Binding Completion, closing out `v0.Y.38.1`.** Settled in `v0.0.38.1`. Requested directly by the user (2026-09-19, "Make the new filters bindable to Mindwaves"), not a pre-planned roadmap milestone - extends `v0.Y.31.1` (MindWaves v1) Installment D's own per-cell MindWave-binding mechanism (`buildParameterField()`/`perCellParameterValue()`, unchanged) from the original five filter parameters to every remaining `v0.Y.36.1` (Deferred Filters) parameter that fits it, closing a gap both of those already-shipped milestones explicitly flagged as deliberate future work.

    - **Fifteen parameters across eleven filter types bind, three remain deliberately excluded, confirmed with the user before implementation**: `speckleDensity`/`speckleIntensity` (SpeckleAdd/DynamicSpeckle, shared), `speckleThresholdDb`, `noiseFloorDb`/`reductionDb`, `crushAmount`, `grainAmountDb`, `feedbackAmount`, `foldGain`, `channelBalance`, `convolveAmount`, `displaceDistance`/`displaceAngleDegrees`, `channelCycleAngleDegrees`, and `reverbMix` all bind. `Convolve`'s own kernel *size*, `GranularNoise`'s own block *size*, and `SpectralReverb`'s own shape parameters (`reverbPreDelayFrames`/`reverbDecayFrames`/`reverbRoomSize`/`reverbDiffusion`/`reverbAbsorption`) do not - each shapes a fixed-size structure (a kernel matrix, a block partition) or a computation spanning many frames/bins at once (a temporal impulse response, a frequency-axis blur kernel), not an independent per-cell value with a well-defined "this cell's own value" the way every bindable parameter has. This is a real, permanent limitation of the per-cell binding mechanism itself, not a deferred future-work item the way the fifteen were before this milestone - confirmed with the user as the correct read over attempting the temporal-cost-and-semantics tradeoff anyway.
    - **Every one of the fifteen varies for free (like `sharpenAmount`, never like the four original kernel-shape parameters)** - none needed the array-building `buildParameterField()`/GPU-dispatch machinery `blurSigma`/`medianSize`/`directionalBlurLength`/`directionalBlurAngle` use; each is either genuinely pixel-local (a post-hoc dry/wet blend after an already-computed, unbound result - `convolveAmount`, `reverbMix`; or a direct per-cell formula with no neighborhood - `displaceDistance`/`displaceAngleDegrees`, `channelCycleAngleDegrees`, matching the design doc's own explicit pixel-local examples) or a parameter an already-existing per-cell loop simply evaluates fresh instead of once (`speckleThresholdDb`, `noiseFloorDb`/`reductionDb`, `crushAmount`, `feedbackAmount`, `foldGain`, `channelBalance`). No new "Varying" GPU-dispatch functions were needed at all for this milestone, unlike Installment D's own four.
    - **`SpeckleAdd`'s own density/intensity bind genuinely per cell; `DynamicSpeckle`'s/`GranularNoise`'s own bind once per block (at the block's own top-left cell), reasoned through rather than asked**: `SpeckleAdd` already decides per-cell whether a hash qualifies as a "hit," so a bound density/intensity varies at that same per-cell granularity for free. `DynamicSpeckle`/`GranularNoise` are both already block-based (a live per-block RNG roll, a per-block hashed offset respectively) - binding their own shared amount finer than the block itself would look visually inconsistent (a smoothly-varying value inside an otherwise blocky effect) and doesn't match either filter's own existing granularity, so both evaluate the bound field once per block instead, applied uniformly across it - the same convention `grainSize()`'s own block-keying-by-top-left-corner already establishes.
    - **The Studio UI mirrors `SpeckleAdd`'s/`DynamicSpeckle`'s own established "duplicated widget, shared field, cross-synced" pattern for their own bind combos too, not just their spin boxes**: `speckleDensityMindWave()`/`speckleIntensityMindWave()` each back two separate combo widgets (one per group), and selecting a MindWave in either immediately pushes the same selection onto its sibling combo (`QSignalBlocker`-guarded), the identical fix `v0.Y.36.1` Installment A's own spin-box cross-sync bug already established for these two types' shared fields - applied here proactively, not found by a failing test this time, since the shape of the bug was already known.
    - **New tests**: `test_filter_configuration.cpp` (Core, 4 new cases - fresh-unbound, bind/unbind, JSON round-trip, lenient old-format load, covering all fifteen at once). `test_filter_application.cpp` (Core, 32 new cases - ceiling/baseline pairs for every parameter, plus "genuinely varies per cell" spot-checks for `displaceDistance`, `reverbMix`, and `speckleDensity`, plus `DynamicSpeckle`'s/`GranularNoise`'s own block-uniform binding behavior). `test_filter_configuration_panel.cpp` (Studio, 5 new cases, generic across all seventeen combo widgets - fresh-unbound, population, change-and-emit, unbind-and-emit, and `setFilterConfiguration()` sync). Full regression: sound-mind-core 687/687 (up from 651), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings.

108. **MindWaves v2, Installment A: Warp + Reduce field operators, plus Reduce wired into Sound Mind Instruments as vibrato/tremolo, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "MindWave Functions"/"Field Operators".** Settled in `v0.0.39.1`. Scoped via two rounds of confirming questions before any code (installment split, Reduce's consumer, drawn-shape authoring approach and step-grid UI for the later installments, and the Instruments-wiring mechanism itself) - the design doc's own Deferred Decision #7 explicitly disclaims having resolved the UI/UX for most of this milestone's scope, the same situation Decision #97's Order/Chaos and Decision #101's/#102's Warp (the filter, an unrelated namesake) were both in.

    - **Warp stores its own source as a nested, embedded `MindWave`, not a `MindWaveId` reference into the project library** - reuses `superpositionStack()`'s own already-proven `std::vector<MindWave>` sized-0-or-1 representation (`warpSourceStack_`), rather than `std::optional<MindWave>`, since only `std::vector`/`std::list`/`std::forward_list` get C++17's incomplete-type dispensation for a class holding a member of its own type; `std::optional` does not. This also sidesteps any possibility of a reference cycle through the project's MindWave library the way a `MindWaveId`-based binding would have to guard against - a warp source is always a fully independent, self-contained value, editable only through its own nested `MindWaveEditor`, one level deep (the same nesting limit the existing superposition-stack editor already established), never a pointer back into anything that could point back at it.
    - **Reduce deliberately collapses frequency only, never time** - a genuine asymmetry reasoned through rather than silently generalized: frequency has a well-defined, bounded range (`[0, binCount)`) to integrate across; time is unbounded, with no canonical "how much time" a time-collapsing Reduce could integrate over. This narrower scope matches the design doc's own "typically frequency" framing directly, rather than reading as an oversight - the same kind of deliberate narrowing Decision #107's own three permanently-excluded filter parameters already established a precedent for flagging explicitly.
    - **The Instruments-wiring insight**: rather than building the separate, larger "operation-relative MindWave binding" architecture a genuine per-note LFO would normally need (every MindWave binding in this codebase today is canvas-space only), a bound vibrato/tremolo MindWave is reduced once per stroke to a plain 1D signal and sampled at each stamp's own `pathT` (0..1 progress along the *whole stroke* - already computed for the stroke's own gradient lookup, not a new concept). This achieves a "varies meaningfully across the stroke" effect without a fresh per-note retrigger, confirmed with the user as an acceptable simplification for now, explicitly distinct from the genuine per-note clock a future dedicated milestone could still add.
    - **`PaintParameterMindWaves` was tried and discarded in favor of a `MindWaveResolver` (a per-id resolver function, matching `LayerContentResolver`'s own existing shape) mid-implementation** - a real design correction, not a stylistic preference: `FilterParameterMindWaves`'s own precedent (a single struct of pre-resolved pointers, built once by `compositor.cpp` before a single `FilterConfiguration` evaluation) doesn't fit `rebuildPaintedContent()`'s own shape, which replays a *history* of `PaintOperation`s on one layer, each carrying its own `InstrumentConfiguration` snapshot from whenever it was painted - two different strokes on the same layer can legitimately be bound to two different MindWaves, so a single externally-supplied pair of pointers for the whole replay would have silently applied one stroke's binding to every other stroke's own replay. `applyPaintOperation()` instead re-resolves each operation's own stored `vibratoMindWave()`/`tremoloMindWave()` id, on every replay, against whatever the resolver returns *right now* - `PaintController` supplies one reading straight from the live `Project`.
    - **New tests**: `test_mind_wave.cpp` (Core, 12 new cases - Warp's displacement/strength/frequency-axis/JSON round-trip/lenient load, Reduce's Slice/Integrate modes, sample-count flooring, `[0, 1]` range). `test_tool_configuration.cpp` (Core, 5 new cases - fresh defaults, vibrato/tremolo binding+depth changes, `clone()`, JSON round-trip, lenient old-format load). `test_paint_application.cpp` (Core, 5 new cases - vibrato bend, tremolo silence/no-op-at-ceiling, resolver-can't-resolve, `rebuildPaintedContent()`'s own per-operation re-resolution). `test_paint_controller.cpp` (Studio, 2 new cases - end-to-end resolver wiring against a live `Project`, live re-resolution on `rebuildLayerContent()` after editing the bound MindWave directly). `test_tool_configuration_panel.cpp` (Studio, 6 new cases - combo population, depth/binding changes and emission, unbind, load-sync, tool-type-switch preservation). `test_mind_waves_panel.cpp` (Studio, 6 new cases - enable/disable, nested editor edits, strength changes, fresh-selection/existing-warp-source load sync). Full regression: sound-mind-core 706/706 (up from 698), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings.

109. **MindWaves v2, Installment B: drawn-shape generator, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "MindWave Functions" ("Drawn shapes").** Settled in `v0.0.39.2`. Scoped via one round of confirming questions before any code (output normalization, loop-length control) - the design doc's own Deferred Decision #7 explicitly disclaims having resolved the interaction design for this generator type; the capture-gesture mechanism itself (mirroring `Warp Selection`'s exact workflow) had already been confirmed with the user during Installment A's own planning pass.

    - **`drawnPath()` stores the captured curve as a plain `Path`, read only for its geometry** - `evaluate()` never consults the path's own `gradient()`, since a MindWave has no notion of color/opacity along its length; only anchors and Bézier handles matter.
    - **Looping reuses `period()`/`phaseRadians()` (confirmed with the user over locking the loop to the path's own recorded duration)** - the same shared-field philosophy `MindWave`'s own class docs already establish for every other generator type (`Envelope`'s oscillation period, `SteppedNoise`'s cycle length, `Spatial`'s ring spacing, `Fractal`'s tile length all already reuse `period()` rather than each declaring their own). The path's own recorded domain span (first node's own axis coordinate to last node's own) is rescaled into `[0, period())` via the exact same `p = (axisPosition/safePeriod) + phaseRadians/(2*pi); p -= floor(p)` formula `SteppedNoise::Stepped`/`Fractal` already use, rather than a bespoke drawn-specific looping formula.
    - **First-crossing rule, not an average or a last-crossing rule** (confirmed with the user): a hand-drawn stroke can loop back on itself (a wiggly vertical line queried on the Time axis, say), making it genuinely non-single-valued as a function of the queried axis. Resolving this by walking the curve's own tessellated polyline from its start (`t=0`) and returning the *first* segment that brackets the queried domain value is deterministic and simple, at the cost of "later" parts of a self-crossing curve being unreachable at that domain position - accepted as the right tradeoff for a hand-drawn shape, where "first thing you drew there" reads as the more natural interpretation than an average of every crossing.
    - **Output normalizes against `Path::bounds()` (the path's own recorded bounding box), not the project's global frequency/time range** (confirmed with the user): "what you drew is what you get" - a small squiggle drawn in one corner of the canvas still uses its own full `[0, 1]` field range, rather than only ever producing a narrow band unless drawn across the entire canvas. `Path::bounds()` was already a tested, existing utility (built for Pick's own hit-testing and Composer Mode's track boxes) reused here unchanged, not a new bounding-box computation.
    - **A real, deliberate approximation flagged rather than silently accepted**: the Frequency axis's own domain (bins) is a *log* function of the raw Hz values a straight path edge linearly interpolates, so the crossing search's linear interpolation between adjacent tessellated polyline points is only locally accurate there (fine at `kDrawnPathSubdivisionsPerEdge = 32` substeps per edge, matching `containsPoint()`'s own established tessellation density, but not exact the way the Time axis's interpolation is, since Time and the path's own other coordinate share the identical smoothstep-eased parametrization along a straight two-anchor edge and so stay exactly linearly related to each other regardless of tessellation density). Caught during testing (a hand-derived "halfway in bins should read halfway along the curve" expectation was measurably off - `0.4138` instead of `0.5`) - the implementation was correct, the test's own assumption wasn't; fixed by testing monotonicity/exact endpoints for the Frequency-axis case instead of an interior numeric prediction, rather than loosening the implementation's own tessellation density to chase an exact match it can't have in closed form.
    - **New tessellation helper (`tessellateOpenPath()`, `mind_wave.cpp`) duplicates, rather than shares, `containsPoint()`'s own polygon tessellation** (`path.cpp`) - deliberately: a drawn shape's own curve is explicitly *not* implicitly closed the way a Lasso boundary is (see `drawnPath()`'s own docs), so the two tessellations differ in exactly the one respect (no wraparound closing edge) that would make a shared implementation more awkward than two small, independent ones. `evaluateCubicBezier()` itself (declared in `paint_application.h`) is reused directly, not duplicated - `mind_wave.cpp` already depended on `paint_application.h` for `frequencyToBinIndex()`/`binIndexToFrequency()` since Installment A, and `path.cpp` already established the identical "depend on `paint_application.h` for this one shared Bézier-math utility" precedent for its own tessellation.
    - **Capture mechanism**: `MindWaveController::setDrawnPath(id, path)` sets both `type()` (to `Drawn`) and `drawnPath()` in one call, leaving every other field (`period()`, `phaseRadians()`, the superposition stack, warp) untouched - mirroring `Warp Selection`'s own precedent of not requiring its target to already be some special pre-existing kind. Wired from a new `MainWindow::usePickedPathAsMindWaveShape()` / **Edit -> Use Picked Path as MindWave Shape** action, reading `ToolPaletteController::selectedPath()` exactly the way `warpSelection()` already does, applied to whichever entry `MindWavesPanel::selectedMindWaveId()` currently reports - no committed-selection requirement, unlike `Warp Selection`'s own extra `hasSelection()` gate, since this applies to a MindWaves library entry, not canvas content.
    - **`MindWaveEditor`'s own `Drawn` group is read-only status text, not editable controls** - consistent with the class's own established "no superposition-stack editing here either" boundary: authoring happens externally (the Pick-and-apply workflow above), this group only reports node count or "nothing captured yet."
    - **No dedicated Studio-level test for `MainWindow::usePickedPathAsMindWaveShape()` itself** - matching the established precedent that its own closest sibling, `warpSelection()`, has no dedicated test either; the real logic each thin wrapper delegates to (`MindWaveController::setDrawnPath()`, `ToolPaletteController::warpSelection()`) is what's actually tested.
    - **New tests**: `test_mind_wave.cpp` (Core, 8 new cases - no-path/single-node neutral fallback, straight two-node crossing at start/middle/wraparound, `period()`-independent loop stretching, first-crossing ambiguity resolution, Frequency-axis bin-domain conversion, zero-width-span fallback, JSON round-trip, lenient old-format load). `test_mind_wave_editor.cpp` (Studio, 3 new cases - group visibility, uncaptured status text, captured node count plus preserved-through-round-trip). `test_mind_wave_controller.cpp` (Studio, 2 new cases - type-switch-and-capture preserving other fields, no-project/unknown-id no-ops). Full regression: sound-mind-core 714/714 (up from 706), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings.

110. **MindWaves v2, Installment C: step-grid generator, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "MindWave Functions" ("Step grids").** Settled in `v0.0.39.3`. No new confirming questions before code - the UI shape ("bare-bones step-count + per-step spin box list") and the underlying representation choice (a plain `std::vector<double>`, no separate count field) were both already settled by direct precedent (`InstrumentConfiguration::harmonicStrengths()`) rather than genuine new ambiguity, unlike Installments A/B's own field operators and drawn-shape generator.

    - **`stepGridValues()` is a distinct concept from `SteppedNoiseShape::Stepped`'s own `stepCount()`, despite the similar "stepped" naming** - `Stepped` is a formula (a quantized *staircase*, deriving `stepCount_` equal levels algorithmically); `StepGrid` is explicit, hand-authored data (each step's own value typed in directly, with no formula behind it at all). Reusing `stepCount()`/`stepCount_` for both would have made one accessor mean two genuinely different things depending on `type()` - unlike `period()`/`phaseRadians()`, which mean the *literal same thing* everywhere they're reused; a new, separately-named field was the correct call, not a shared one.
    - **No separate step-count field** - `stepGridValues().size()` *is* the count, the same "the vector's own size already is the count, don't duplicate it in a second field that has to stay in sync" reasoning `InstrumentConfiguration::harmonicStrengths()` already established. `MindWaveEditor`'s own `stepGridCountSpinBox_` is a pure UI convenience (resizing the row list to match), never itself stored.
    - **Looping reuses `period()`/`phaseRadians()`, the exact same formula `SteppedNoiseShape::Stepped` already uses** (`p = (axisPosition/safePeriod) + phaseRadians/(2*pi); p -= floor(p); index = clamp(floor(p * count), 0, count-1)`) - one period divided into `stepGridValues().size()` equal-width steps, each returned verbatim. No new looping mechanism was designed; the existing one already fit exactly.
    - **Defaults to a four-step rising staircase (`{0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0}`), not an empty vector** - an empty `stepGridValues()` would be silently neutral (`evaluate()`'s own defensive fallback returns `0.5` for it, to avoid indexing an empty vector), which would read as broken rather than "not yet configured" the moment someone previews a fresh `StepGrid` MindWave - the same "visibly does something the moment it's bound or previewed" default philosophy `MindWave`'s own constructor already establishes for every other generator type.
    - **A real implementation bug caught by the regression suite, not a test-assumption error this time** (unlike Decision #109's own Frequency-axis case): `MindWaveEditor`'s own `kGeneratorTypes` combo-population array was updated for `Drawn` in Installment B but the equivalent addition for `StepGrid` was missed in the first pass here - `generatorTypeCombo_->findData(...)` for `StepGrid` silently found no match, so `setCurrentIndex()` was a no-op and the editor's own type stayed `Periodic`. Caught immediately by `switchingToStepGridShowsOnlyItsOwnGroupWithTheDefaultFourSteps()` failing full regression (`editor.mindWave().type()` read `0` instead of `6`) - isolated by temporarily reducing `sound-mind-studio/tests/main.cpp` to run only `MindWaveEditorTest` (via a file-based `-o file,txt` reporter, since this environment's console PASS/FAIL output doesn't reliably reach redirected stdout for the full multi-class binary) to get a readable per-test failure message, then reverted after diagnosis. Fixed by adding the missing array entry; a lesson to explicitly grep for every array a new enum value needs to appear in, not just the ones a test happens to already cover.
    - **New tests**: `test_mind_wave.cpp` (Core, 6 new cases - default four-step staircase, verbatim per-step values across one period including wraparound, phase-offset step shifting, empty-vector neutral fallback, JSON round-trip, lenient old-format load). `test_mind_wave_editor.cpp` (Studio, 4 new cases - group visibility with default steps shown, count-driven row resize, value-change emission, load-sync of count and each step's own value). Full regression: sound-mind-core 720/720 (up from 714), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings.

111. **MindWaves v2, Installment D: Continuous Controls generator, closing out `v0.Y.39.1` in full, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "Continuous Controls".** Settled in `v0.0.39.4`. Scoped via one round of confirming questions before any code (whether Shape genuinely blends `evaluate()`-level output or snaps across a discrete progression of existing types; whether the three knob positions themselves persist on `MindWave`) - the design doc's own text ("two or three continuous controls... that smoothly sweep through a whole family of the generator types and parameter combinations") is deliberately non-prescriptive about the actual mechanism, and Deferred Decision #7 explicitly disclaims a real interaction-design pass for MindWaves generally.

    - **A genuinely new evaluation path was chosen over a discrete-progression front door** (confirmed with the user, reversing this Decision's own initial recommendation): `MindWave`'s own generator types/waveforms are discrete enums with no interpolation between them, so a literal continuous blend needs new `evaluate()`-level logic, not just a mechanism that snaps between existing settings at threshold points the way, say, `changeToolType()`'s carry-over logic snaps a `ToolConfiguration` between concrete subtypes. `GeneratorType::Continuous`'s own case computes a clean sine value and a fractal-noise value at the same point and linearly interpolates between them by `continuousShape()` - a real, if simple, new formula, not a redirection to existing formulas.
    - **Skew and Character needed no equivalent compromise** - both map directly onto already-continuous quantities (a phase offset; an added noise sample's own amplitude), so only `continuousShape()`'s own generator-family axis had a discreteness problem to solve at all. `continuousSkew()` is a *second*, dedicated phase bias, deliberately not reusing the shared `phaseRadians()` field every generator already has - the design doc frames Skew as one of Continuous's own three specific dials, not a rename of a field every other generator type also exposes.
    - **All three knobs persist on `MindWave` itself, confirmed with the user over Studio-only ephemeral UI state** - `evaluate()` never reads `continuousShape()`/`continuousSkew()`/`continuousCharacter()` through any separate "last-set knob" cache (the blend formula reads the same three persisted fields directly, so there's no redundant storage to keep in sync), but persisting them at all means reselecting a `Continuous` MindWave or reopening the project redisplays the three dials exactly where they were left - matching every other MindWave control's own persistence, rather than reverting to a default position each time despite the underlying field values being unchanged.
    - **Character's own turbulence layer is independent of Shape's own blend, by design** - a separate, finer-grained noise sample (a tenth of `noiseScale()`'s own lattice size) is added on top of the shape/skew result, scaled by `continuousCharacter()`, rather than being folded into the shape blend itself. This means turbulence can be dialled in even at `continuousShape() == 0` (a "clean" sine cycle with rough character added), matching the design doc's own framing of Character as a distinct dial from Shape, not a redundant one.
    - **A second real implementation bug caught by the regression suite** (the same class of mistake as Decision #110's own `StepGrid` omission, not a new kind of error): `MindWaveEditor`'s own `kGeneratorTypes` array update for `Continuous` was made correctly this time, but the three new spin boxes' own initial displayed values were never explicitly seeded from `wave_`'s own defaults at construction - `continuousShapeSpinBox_`'s/`continuousCharacterSpinBox_`'s own Qt-default `0.0` happened to coincidentally match `MindWave`'s own defaults, masking the gap, but `continuousSkewSpinBox_`'s own Qt-default `0.0` did not match `continuousSkew()`'s own real default of `0.5` - caught by `switchingToContinuousShowsOnlyItsOwnGroupWithTheDefaultKnobPositions()` failing full regression, isolated the same way (a temporary `main.cpp` reduction to just `MindWaveEditorTest`, reverted after diagnosis). Fixed by explicitly seeding all three spin boxes from `wave_`'s own current values at the end of the constructor, the same "seeded from wave_'s own defaults directly" pattern the step-grid rows already established in Installment C - a second, independent instance of the same underlying lesson: a control whose own "correct by coincidence" initial value isn't explicitly asserted anywhere will eventually be the one that silently isn't.
    - **New tests**: `test_mind_wave.cpp` (Core, 8 new cases - default neutral knobs, clean unbiased sine at shape=0, skew phase-biasing, shape sweeping away from the clean value, a linear-interpolation consistency check between the shape=0/shape=1/shape=0.5 results, character adding turbulence independent of shape, JSON round-trip, lenient old-format load). `test_mind_wave_editor.cpp` (Studio, 3 new cases - group visibility with default knob positions shown, per-knob change-and-emit, load-sync of all three knobs). Full regression: sound-mind-core 728/728 (up from 720), sound-mind-studio all 35 QTest classes passing. Doxygen: 0 warnings. This closes out the `v0.Y.39.1` (MindWaves v2) milestone in full.

112. **Chords/Arpeggiator/Sequencer, Installment A: the core editable-sequence architecture, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "Chords/Arpeggiator/Sequencer".** Settled in `v0.0.40.1`. Scoped via one round of confirming questions before any code (the sequence-notation format direction - `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s own Deferred Decision #4 - and the installment split/order) - genuinely large, new architectural territory (a *new kind* of `Operation` that stays editable after logging, unlike every existing subtype which bakes into pixels once applied), on par with MindWaves v1/Sound Mind Instruments, which the roadmap's own convention already scopes this way.

    - **Deferred Decision #4 resolved: a custom notation in Sound Mind's own units, not ABC** (confirmed with the user) - ABC notation is fundamentally pitch-*name*-based (A-G, accidentals, octave marks) with no native way to express an arbitrary Hz value, and Sound Mind's own canvas is a continuous frequency axis, not fixed to 12-TET. Stretching ABC to cover raw Hz would mean abusing its microtonal extensions in a way no real ABC tool would understand - a de facto custom format wearing ABC's syntax, defeating the actual point of reusing an established notation. The notation itself (Installment C) isn't built yet; this decision only settles the *direction*, ahead of that installment's own design pass.
    - **`NoteEvent` is deliberately just pitch and timing** (`startTimeSeconds`/`durationSeconds`/`frequencyHz`), matching the design doc's own literal "a note event... is a pitch and a timing" definition - no per-note color/opacity field, unlike, say, a `GradientStop`. Every note in one `SequenceOperation` shares that operation's own single `config()` (`ToolConfiguration::defaultGradient()`), the same way a whole painted stroke already shares one gradient along its length, rather than inventing a new per-note color concept the design doc never asks for.
    - **The actual "stays editable, no repainting" mechanism, reasoned through before any code**: `Operation`'s own base class already establishes that every subtype is immutable once logged (its copy/move constructors are `= delete`d) and that an "edit" is a *new* instance appended with `supersedes()` pointing at the original (`Operation::supersedes()`'s own docs, and `translatedCopy()`'s own precedent for Pick's move gesture). `SequenceOperation` follows this exactly - no `setNotes()`, no mutable `notes()` accessor - so re-voicing/re-timing (Installment B's own job to expose as a UI gesture) was already architecturally settled by this installment: construct a new `SequenceOperation` with edited `notes()`, append it superseding the old one, and `rebuildPaintedContent()`'s own existing replay-from-the-log mechanism does the rest, with zero new machinery needed for "don't repaint."
    - **Every note reuses `applyPaintOperation()`'s own existing per-tool-type rendering entirely, via a throwaway per-note `PaintOperation`** - `applySequenceOperation()` (`sequence_application.h`/`.cpp`) builds a single-tap `Path` (`durationSeconds() <= 0`) or a straight horizontal two-node `Path` (a held note) per `NoteEvent`, wraps it in a `PaintOperation` with a throwaway id (never logged, never observed - purely a vehicle for `applyPaintOperation()`'s own required argument shape) and `config().clone()`, then calls `applyPaintOperation()` directly. No per-tool-type logic was written for `SequenceOperation` at all - a Sound Mind Instrument note gets vibrato/tremolo, a Mind Shot/Mind Grain note gets its own native-size stamp, a plain Procedural note gets its own falloff-blended blob, all for free, exactly matching the design doc's own "anything paintable can be the instrument a sequence plays through."
    - **A pre-existing gap found in `WarpOperation` (`v0.Y.35.1`), not fixed here, flagged for a future cleanup pass**: while designing `SequenceOperation`'s own replay wiring, `WarpOperation` turned out to have never actually been wired into `rebuildPaintedContent()`'s own dispatch at all (`applyWarpOperation()` is defined, fully tested at the Core level, but never called from any production Studio code path) - meaning a `WarpOperation`, once logged via Edit -> Warp Selection, is never actually re-applied on a layer rebuild (undo/redo, a fresh project reload) the way every other operation type already is. It also has no `OperationLog`-level JSON round-trip test of its own (`test_operation_log.cpp` covers `PaintOperation`/`FillOperation`/`PasteOperation`, but no `WarpOperation` case exists). Unrelated to this milestone's own scope and out of "no scope creep" bounds to fix here - noted so it isn't lost, and `SequenceOperation` was deliberately checked against both gaps directly (it *is* wired into `rebuildPaintedContent()`, and *does* have real `OperationLog`-level round-trip tests, including a superseded-by-a-re-voice scenario) rather than copying an unverified precedent.
    - **No Studio UI in this installment at all, per the confirmed installment split** - there is currently no way to construct a `SequenceOperation` from the Studio (no Chord Generator, no notation editor - both are later installments' own job), so this installment is Core-only, its own correctness resting entirely on its test suite rather than a manual demo.
    - **New tests**: `test_sequence_operation.cpp` (Core, new file, 8 cases - construction/id/target-layer/notes/config, supersedes presence/absence, `bounds()`'s own union-of-notes and empty-sequence cases, `translatedCopy()`'s own time-shift/config-preservation/bin-based-frequency-shift). `test_sequence_application.cpp` (Core, new file, 5 cases - zero-duration single-tap, held-note horizontal band, block-chord multi-note stamping, empty-sequence no-op, rendering through a non-Procedural tool type to confirm no per-tool-type logic was needed). `test_paint_application.cpp` (Core, 2 new cases - `rebuildPaintedContent()`'s own new `SequenceOperation` dispatch branch, and the "re-voice via supersedes needs no repainting" claim end to end). `test_operation_log.cpp` (Core, 2 new cases - JSON round-trip with notes/config intact, and a superseded-by-a-re-voice scenario surviving the round-trip). Full regression: sound-mind-core 745/745 (up from 728). Doxygen: 0 warnings.

113. **Chords/Arpeggiator/Sequencer, Installment B: the Chord Generator UI and Chord Overlay, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "Chords/Arpeggiator/Sequencer"/"Chord Overlay".** Settled in `v0.0.40.2`. Scoped via one round of confirming questions before any code (whether a canvas click should set a chord's own frequency, the way every other paint tool's click does, or only its start time, given the panel's Root/Octave already fully determine pitch) - a genuine, new interaction-model fork, not a detail resolvable by precedent alone.

    - **Click places only the chord's own start time, never its pitch - confirmed with the user, the one real fork this installment raised.** Every other tool mode (`Paint`/`Pick`/`Select`/`Path`) uses a click's *full* 2D position; `ChordStamp` (the new `CanvasWidget::ToolMode` value) deliberately uses only its time-axis half, since the Chord Generator panel's own Root/Octave controls already fully determine every note's own pitch - using the click's frequency too would either be redundant or (worse) silently override the panel's own settings. This is a genuinely new interaction pattern in this codebase (no existing tool ignores half of a click's position), not a reuse of the existing paint-stroke path - `CanvasWidget::chordStampRequested(TimeFrequencyPoint)` is a new signal, deliberately carrying the *full* point anyway (matching every other tool mode's own signal shape, for one uniform conversion path), with only `.timeSeconds` actually consulted downstream.
    - **A compiled-in chord/arpeggio data module, ported from the legacy Python Studio's own `chord_library.py`/`chords.json`/`arpeggiator.py` (`chord_generator.h`/`.cpp`, new Core files)**: `chordsInCategory()` (36 chords across the design doc's own five categories, as static C++ data - not a runtime-loaded file, matching this codebase's own fixed-vocabulary-table convention already established for e.g. `BrushTipShape`) and `arpeggioIndexSequence()` (the legacy's own nine order presets, each a faithful port of `arpeggiator.py`'s own per-preset algorithm - `OutsideIn`/`InsideOut` in particular keep the legacy's own names despite each actually sorting by the *opposite* edge-distance direction its name implies, a faithful port of already-named, already-shipped behavior rather than a fresh design). **`Random` is a deliberate behavior change**: the legacy's own `build_sequence()` called `random.shuffle()` directly, non-reproducibly; `arpeggioIndexSequence()` instead takes an explicit `randomSeed` and shuffles via a seeded `std::mt19937_64`, matching this codebase's own established "anything seeded replays bit-for-bit" rule (the same one `MindWave`'s own seeded generators already follow) - a real, deliberate divergence from the legacy precedent, not an oversight.
    - **`frequencyForMidiNote()` added to `music_theory.h`/`.cpp`** - the missing exact inverse of the pre-existing `noteNameForFrequency()`, sharing its own `69 = A4` convention; `buildChordNotes()` uses it to resolve `rootMidiNote + interval` to real Hz for every note in a chosen chord.
    - **`buildChordNotes()` resolves a `ChordGeneratorParams` into real `NoteEvent`s** - Block mode stamps every note at the same start time; Arpeggio mode steps through `arpeggioIndexSequence()`'s own sequence at `(60/bpm) * stepBeats` seconds per step, `repeats` full cycles, each note held for `stepBeats`'s own step duration times `noteDurationFraction`. "Voicing" (the design doc's own "choose a root note, a chord category... and a specific voicing" wording) is resolved as "which named chord within the category" - confirmed with the user, matching the legacy's own only granularity level; no separate inversion/octave-doubling axis. The legacy's own separate "Stamp Length" repeat-the-whole-passage feature (Repeat N times/For Y seconds/Fill layer) and its "Instrument" group (MIDI program/velocity - no clean `NoteEvent` equivalent, which deliberately has no per-note intensity) were both dropped rather than ported, reasoned through rather than asked about: no other Sound Mind Studio tool has a "repeat N times" stamping convenience, and velocity has nothing in this codebase's own model to attach to.
    - **No instrument picker of its own in `ChordGeneratorPanel` - confirmed as the right scope by re-deriving it from `SequenceOperation`'s own "anything paintable can be the instrument" framing, not asked about.** `ChordGeneratorController::stampAt()` clones whatever `PaintController::toolConfiguration()` (the Tool Configuration Panel's own current selection) currently is into the new `SequenceOperation`'s own `config()` - switching the Tool Configuration Panel between Procedural/Instrument/Mind Shot/Mind Grain changes what a stamped chord sounds like, exactly as it already does for an ordinary painted stroke, with zero new picker UI needed.
    - **Re-voicing/re-timing an already-stamped chord needed zero new Studio-level code - verified by dedicated research (a foreground `Explore` subagent over `pick_controller.cpp`) before any Installment B code was written, not assumed from Installment A's own docs.** `SequenceOperation::translatedCopy()` (Installment A) already makes any picked sequence movable through `PickController`'s own fully generic move gesture - `pick()`'s candidacy check, `continueMove()`'s live preview, and `endMove()`'s commit all dispatch through the base `Operation*` with no subtype-specific branching at all, so dragging a picked chord left/right re-times it and up/down uniformly re-voices it, for free. The one related gap the same research surfaced and this installment deliberately leaves open: `PickController::selectedConfiguration()` still special-cases `dynamic_cast<const PaintOperation*>` and returns `nullptr` for a picked `SequenceOperation`, so its own instrument can't yet be reopened/re-pointed in the Tool Configuration Panel the way a picked `PaintOperation`'s can - a distinct, smaller feature, flagged for a later pass rather than folded into this one.
    - **The Chord Overlay is a genuinely separate overlay from the Frequency Grid, not a temporary addition to its state** - confirmed by the design doc's own literal "independent of, and in addition to, the general frequency grid" wording. `CanvasWidget::setChordPreview(std::vector<double>)`/`drawChordPreview()` are new, parallel to `setFrequencyGridConfig()`/`drawGrid()` rather than routed through `FrequencyGridConfig`'s own `customFrequenciesEnabled` mechanism, which - despite being visually almost identical (a set of Hz values drawn as horizontal lines) - is user-owned, persistent-within-session state a live chord preview has no business perturbing (or being perturbed by).
    - **`ToolPaletteController` gained a fifth owned sub-controller (`ChordGeneratorController`), not a sixth `MainWindow`-level wiring surface** - constructed the same way `PickController`/`SelectionController`/`PathController` already are (sharing `paintController_`'s own tool configuration), with its `previewChanged()`/`contentChanged()` wired internally the same "internal wiring, one merged signal out" treatment the other three already get. It does *not* hold a pointer to `ChordGeneratorPanel` the way it holds `toolConfigurationPanel_`, though - the panel's `paramsChanged()` connects directly from `MainWindow` instead (`setChordParams()`/`stampChord()` are its own two new public forwarding methods) - deliberate: nothing else inside `ToolPaletteController` needs to reach into that panel the way Pick's own "reload the picked config" flow needs `toolConfigurationPanel_`.
    - **`ChordStamp` needed no cancel-on-deactivate handling, unlike Paint/Pick/Select/Path** - its own single-press gesture (the same "no drag/release counterpart" shape `Path`'s own `pathNodePlaced()` already established) has no in-progress state to cancel in the first place, so `MainWindow::setChordModeEnabled()` is the one toolbar-toggle handler among the five with no `cancel*()` call of its own.
    - **New tests**: `test_music_theory.cpp` (Core, 4 new cases - `frequencyForMidiNote()`'s own reference-note/inverse-of-`noteNameForFrequency()`/retuned-reference/round-trip behavior). `test_chord_generator.cpp` (Core, new file, 16 cases - category/chord-table contents, every `arpeggioIndexSequence()` preset's own worked example at `n=3`/`n=4`, `Custom`'s verbatim-or-ascending-fallback behavior, `Random`'s own seeded-reproducibility and permutation-validity, `buildChordNotes()`'s own Block/Arpeggio/repeats/retuned-reference/out-of-range-index behavior). `test_chord_generator_controller.cpp` (Studio, new file, 8 cases - default-params preview, `previewChanged()` emission, preview dedup/ordering, `stampAt()`'s own no-project/out-of-range/success/tool-configuration-cloning/start-time-resolution behavior). `test_chord_generator_panel.cpp` (Studio, new file, 8 cases - default params, category-switch chord-combo rebuild, root/octave resolution, Block-vs-Arpeggio group visibility, Custom/Random-only control visibility, custom-sequence parsing, subdivision-to-`stepBeats` mapping). Full regression: sound-mind-core 765/765 (up from 745), sound-mind-studio full suite passing (exit code 0 across two independent full runs; this environment's own QTest text logger produces no PASS/FAIL detail once piped through this session's own tooling - a pre-existing, documented limitation, see `sound-mind-studio/tests/CMakeLists.txt`'s own comment - so exit code, not per-test output, is this installment's own regression evidence). Doxygen: 0 warnings.
    - **Deviation from `CLAUDE.md`'s own tests-first step, flagged rather than silently let stand**: the Core-layer work (chord data, arpeggio math, `frequencyForMidiNote()`) followed tests-first exactly; the Studio-layer work (`ChordGeneratorController`/`ChordGeneratorPanel`) did not - implementation was written first, with `test_chord_generator_controller.cpp`/`test_chord_generator_panel.cpp` added immediately afterward, before this installment was considered done, rather than before the classes existed. Full coverage exists either way, but the ordering itself departed from the mandated workflow.

114. **Chords/Arpeggiator/Sequencer, Installment C: the generalized text notation, per `docs/sound-mind-design.md`'s "Chords/Arpeggiator/Sequencer" and its own long-open sequence-notation Deferred Decision.** Settled in `v0.0.40.3`. Closes out this milestone in full (Installments A/B/C all done; MIDI-controller driving remains explicitly deferred, per the design doc's own "desirable, not a hard requirement"). Scoped via one round of confirming questions before any code (a compact, ABC-inspired custom grammar vs. a JSON-like structured list) - a genuine, consequential syntax fork, not a detail resolvable by precedent alone, the same bar Installment B's own click-placement question met.

    - **Grammar confirmed with the user: compact and ABC-inspired, over a JSON-like structured list** - `<pitch>:<duration>` note tokens (`A4:0.5`, `440:1.0`), `z<duration>` rests (ABC's own reserved silence letter, chosen specifically to stay unambiguous alongside the `A`-`G` note names), and `+`-joined tokens for simultaneous notes (`A4:0.5+C#5:0.5+E5:0.5`, a block chord). Duration is plain seconds by default, or beats via a `b` suffix (`1b`), resolved the same `(60/bpm) * value` way `ChordGeneratorParams::stepBeats` already does. Implemented in new Core files `sequence_notation.h`/`.cpp` (`parseSequenceNotation()`/`sequenceNotationFor()`).
    - **Sequential timing, not explicit per-note timestamps - a deliberate simplification confirmed alongside the grammar itself, not silently assumed.** Each token's own start time is a pure function of everything before it: the first token starts at the caller-supplied `startTimeSeconds`, and every later token starts exactly when the previous one's own *longest* note (or, for a rest, its own duration) ends. There is no `@time` syntax for deliberate overlap between non-simultaneous tokens - a rest is the only way to leave a gap, a `+`-joined group the only way to overlap notes. This keeps the parser itself simple (no need to reconcile an explicit timestamp against the running total) and keeps hand-written notation legible (nothing to keep consistent by hand across edits), at the cost of not being able to express two independent, non-simultaneous voices with arbitrary relative timing in one string - judged an acceptable simplification for what this installment's own scope actually asks for (a single written sequence, not multi-voice counterpoint).
    - **`sequenceNotationFor()` (the serializer) always renders raw Hz and plain-seconds durations, never a note name or a beats-suffixed value, even when parsed from text that used either** - a `NoteEvent`'s own `frequencyHz` is exact, but "the nearest note name" is lossy (see `noteNameForFrequency()`'s own docs), and a `NoteEvent`'s own `durationSeconds` has already lost whatever `bpm` a beats value was originally resolved against. Rendering both canonically (rather than guessing which form "round-trips close enough") means parsing the result back with *any* `bpm`/`referenceHz` always reproduces the same `NoteEvent`s exactly - verified directly by this module's own round-trip tests - at the cost of the rendered text not necessarily matching what a human originally typed.
    - **`ChordGeneratorController` gained a second, independent input source (`setNotation()`) alongside the existing `setParams()`, with `previewFrequenciesHz()`/`stampAt()` dispatching on whichever was set most recently** - re-derived from first principles rather than bolting on a parallel signal/controller pair: since both a built chord and a parsed notation sequence resolve to the exact same shape (a `std::vector<NoteEvent>`, stamped the exact same way), `CanvasWidget`/`MainWindow`/Pick's own move gesture need zero awareness of which source produced a given `SequenceOperation` - matching the design doc's own literal "the same underlying notation... generated the same way a chord is" framing. Invalid notation text is caught inside `resolveNotes()` and resolved to an empty result (`previewFrequenciesHz()` empty, `stampAt()` a no-op) rather than thrown across the Qt signal/slot boundary - the same "nothing happens by accident" fallback `buildChordNotes()`'s own out-of-range-index handling already establishes.
    - **`ChordGeneratorPanel` gained an "Input Mode" combo (Chord Builder / Custom Notation), restructuring its own existing controls into a `chordBuilderGroup_` shown/hidden as one unit** - alongside a new `notationGroup_` (a plain text box, its own BPM/Reference Hz spin boxes, self-contained rather than reading live `ProjectSettings`, matching `ChordGeneratorParams::bpm`/`referenceHz`'s own existing independent-defaults precedent). **Live, inline validation as you type**: every text edit calls `sound_mind::core::parseSequenceNotation()` directly (independent of `ChordGeneratorController`, which needs no such feedback path since it already degrades gracefully on its own) purely to drive an error label - a real parse error is visible immediately, well before the user ever tries to click-stamp anything.
    - **A real, caught-before-commit mistake**: the first working version of `emitNotationChanged()` discarded `parseSequenceNotation()`'s own `[[nodiscard]]` return value (called only for its side effect of throwing on invalid input) - caught immediately by an MSVC `C4834` warning during the very next build, not by a runtime failure or a missed test; fixed with an explicit `[[maybe_unused]]` binding rather than suppressing the warning class.
